I don't really think about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like not following your meal plan would lead to a loss of control in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I... don't really see a connection there. I just prefer to stick to my routine. I don't think altering it would... ruin the rest of my life or anything. It's just how I prefer to do things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious about losing control in any areas of your life beyond just your eating habits?\n\nPatient: I... no. Not really. I don't think I get anxious about things like that. I just... I like things to be orderly. In my line of work, you have to keep things organized, so... that's just how I operate. I don't really sit around worrying about losing control.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any fears about things spiraling out of control in your life outside of your eating habits?\n\nPatient: No. I... I think I just said that, really. I don't have fears about things spiraling. Everything is fine. I just... I don't spend my time worrying about everything falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations in your life, outside of eating, where you feel like things might get out of control?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like you're asking me the exact same question again. I really don't know how else to answer it. No. There aren't. I manage my life just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight has any impact on how attractive you feel?\n\nPatient: I suppose... well, yes. I mean, I know I'm an older woman. I'm 63, I'm not trying to look young or anything like that. But... I still judge how well I'm handling myself by how my clothes fit. If my waist and hips look... contained. If they don't, I just... I don't feel good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you less attractive?\n\nPatient: I... yes. I suppose so. If the scale goes up even a pound... say, after a normal dinner or going out to a restaurant... I feel this sharp panic. It just feels like I've lost control, and I... I need to correct it the next day.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight mostly about feeling fat?\n\nPatient: I... I think I just explained that. It's not really about using the word 'fat.' It's just... like I just mentioned, if the scale goes up even a single pound after a meal out, it brings on that panic. That feeling that I'm losing my grip and have to fix it the next day. I don't really know how else to describe it to you.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight more about feeling like you're losing control or about feeling less attractive?\n\nPatient: I... well, like I just said, it's mostly the control. If the scale is up even a pound after a dinner out, I get that sharp panic. It's just... feeling like I've lost control of things and I have to correct it. I don't really imagine it being higher than what it actually says.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is mostly about how your body looks?\n\nPatient: I... If things deviate and I have a normal dinner, or eat at a restaurant... and the scale is up even a pound the next morning... I get this sharp panic. It just feels like I've lost control of things. And then I feel like I need to correct it the next day. So I prefer to just stick to the plan.\n\nDoctor: Is your meal plan mainly to control your weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose. I mean... I know I’m an older woman. I’m not trying to look young or anything like that. But... I do still judge how well I’m handling myself by... well, by whether my waist and hips look contained in my clothes. It just tells me I'm keeping things in order.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. Like I mentioned a moment ago... if I eat a normal dinner out somewhere and the scale is up even a single pound the next day... I get that sharp panic. It just tells me I've lost control and I have to correct it. So... yes. I prefer to avoid that.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that gaining weight would make you unhealthy?\n\nPatient: I don't... no, not really. I don't really think about it in terms of my health. It's not... I haven't really focused on that part of it. It's just what I said before.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: It's... drifted down some. More than my regular doctor likes, I suppose. But... well, when I look at the chart, I just find myself thinking that a little lower would be... a cleaner number. Just more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I don't... really see it that way. My physician tells me it's medically unsafe, but... I think she's overstating it. I'm still working. I run my own errands, I manage my life just fine. I'm functioning. So... no, I don't really think it's an issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are the only thing in your life that you can control?\n\nPatient: I... I wouldn't say that. I manage a lot of things. My work, my house... I have a handle on my life. It's just... this is one part of it. I don't really think about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like not following your meal plan would lead to a loss of control in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I... don't really see a connection there. I just prefer to stick to my routine. I don't think altering it would... ruin the rest of my life or anything. It's just how I prefer to do things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious about losing control in any areas of your life beyond just your eating habits?\n\nPatient: I... no. Not really. I don't think I get anxious about things like that. I just... I like things to be orderly. In my line of work, you have to keep things organized, so... that's just how I operate. I don't really sit around worrying about losing control.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any fears about things spiraling out of control in your life outside of your eating habits?\n\nPatient: No. I... I think I just said that, really. I don't have fears about things spiraling. Everything is fine. I just... I don't spend my time worrying about everything falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations in your life, outside of eating, where you feel like things might get out of control?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like you're asking me the exact same question again. I really don't know how else to answer it. No. There aren't. I manage my life just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight has any impact on how attractive you feel?\n\nPatient: I suppose... well, yes. I mean, I know I'm an older woman. I'm 63, I'm not trying to look young or anything like that. But... I still judge how well I'm handling myself by how my clothes fit. If my waist and hips look... contained. If they don't, I just... I don't feel good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you less attractive?\n\nPatient: I... yes. I suppose so. If the scale goes up even a pound... say, after a normal dinner or going out to a restaurant... I feel this sharp panic. It just feels like I've lost control, and I... I need to correct it the next day.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight mostly about feeling fat?\n\nPatient: I... I think I just explained that. It's not really about using the word 'fat.' It's just... like I just mentioned, if the scale goes up even a single pound after a meal out, it brings on that panic. That feeling that I'm losing my grip and have to fix it the next day. I don't really know how else to describe it to you.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight more about feeling like you're losing control or about feeling less attractive?\n\nPatient: I... well, like I just said, it's mostly the control. If the scale is up even a pound after a dinner out, I get that sharp panic. It's just... feeling like I've lost control of things and I have to correct it. That's really all I can say about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is higher than it actually is?\n\nPatient: I... no, I don't think so. I just go by the number on the scale. I don't really imagine it being higher than what it actually says.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is mostly about how your body looks?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like I just answered that. It's not really about the mirror, or how my body looks. It's just... the number on the scale, and keeping that where it should be. I don't really have anything else to add to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body is a normal shape and size when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I mean... I know I'm an older woman. I'm not trying to look young or anything like that. But... I guess I just judge how well I'm handling myself by... by whether my waist and hips look contained in my clothes. That's really all I look for.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I'm 63, I'm not trying to look young or anything like that. But... I still judge how well I'm handling myself by how my clothes fit. If my waist and hips look... contained. If they don't, I just... I don't feel good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you less attractive?\n\nPatient: I... yes. I suppose so. If the scale goes up even a pound... say, after a normal dinner or going out to a restaurant... I feel this sharp panic. It just feels like I've lost control, and I... I need to correct it the next day.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight mostly about feeling fat?\n\nPatient: I... I think I just explained that. It's not really about using the word 'fat.' It's just... like I just mentioned, if the scale goes up even a single pound after a meal out, it brings on that panic. That feeling that I'm losing my grip and have to fix it the next day. I don't really know how else to describe it to you.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight more about feeling like you're losing control or about feeling less attractive?\n\nPatient: I... well, like I just said, it's mostly the control. If the scale is up even a pound after a dinner out, I get that sharp panic. It's just... feeling like I've lost control of things and I have to correct it. That's really all I can say about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is higher than it actually is?\n\nPatient: I... no, I don't think so. I just go by the number on the scale. I don't really imagine it being higher than what it actually says.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is mostly about how your body looks?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like I just answered that. It's not really about the mirror, or how my body looks. It's just... the number on the scale, and keeping that where it should be. I don't really have anything else to add to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body is a normal shape and size when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I mean... I know I'm an older woman. I'm not trying to look young or anything like that. But... I guess I just judge how well I'm handling myself by... by whether my waist and hips look contained in my clothes. That's really all I look for.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body shape or size is abnormal when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: No, I... I wouldn't say abnormal. I don't really stare at myself looking for things like that. Like I said, I just check that my clothes fit the way they're supposed to. I don't really dwell on it beyond that."
